 gCLUSJVE: PRIVATE TALK WITH LT. COLONEL JOHN O'MARA, ~EPUTY
   MMANDER. INTELLIGENCE,CONFIRMS THE EXISTENCE OF "FL'tINO
 SAUCERS" -- TRUTH MA Y BE BARED SOON AS "TECHNICAL DATA" REACH]
 THE AIR FORCE. AIR FORCE PLANS COOPERATION WITH PUBLIC.
   The greatest story involving Earth and Space may .oon be told. The "sUence"
   group -- that official fear-facti-:-n oC the Air force -- who Cor years fought to '
   keep the truth from the public has now lost its anaconda-hold on accumulative
   evidence. Stimulated by recent events. the triumphant fearless-faction oC the
 . Air Force will take the benighted public in~ confidence. Aa Col.O'Mara iuti-
, mated: we want to cooperate and dispense with the misc'onceptlon that "flying' -
   saucers" do not exiat. Here is the essence of Col. O'Mara's statements. 9/Z1/54:

                     THE CINCINNATI PAINT INCIDENT -- A PARALLEL PHENOMENON?
                     Residents in Cincinnati, Sept. II, '54 had similar experiences. One Albed
                     Allgeyer came home from work to find the bright yellow trim on his two-sto
                     house stained to a "washed-out brown, almost black color." The stains woul
                     !"..!b oft. without taking the hard top coat of paint.. too. It looked like SQmeont
                     had uled a blow-torch, and in some placn the stains appeared in dirty bro~
                     rivulets. Allgeyer was certain that the stains weren't there the night.befort
                     So was Howard May, neighbor, who received similar treatment. Hi. house
                     trimmed in yellow too, al is another house across the street where stains
                     appeared.

                  On Sept. ZZ. I talked with Allgeyer again and learned that the Kettering La.b I
                  University of Cincinnati had taken air samples as well as specimens of the a
l                 fected paint. A Dr. Hughes explained that the mystery was caused by the ch
                  cal reaction of hydrogen sulphide which could attack the lead in certain paint
'-                The Lab averred that the chemical probably emanated from the Millcreek Va
                  which runs near Allgeyer's home, and which has since affected other homes
     ,.    ,      the area. Although a Proctor and Gamble doctor who examined Allgeyer tol,
          -- _ .. him that the chemical mentioned is not injurioul to people, Allgeyer, five dl
                  after the incident comnlained of extreme swelling of both hands and eye lids.
                  Described as some kind of allergy -- not hives, they offered no explanation.

                     ••• and the possible answer to these phenomena. Write Meade Layne, Direc

        Editor's Note: This incident and Case 14 transcends any notion that "saucer"
        vehicles az:e. confined to earth's atmosphere, therefore U. S. or R'.~ssian origi
        Unless these governments are secretly conducting interplanetaq" travel, Case

        point.                                                 ~

        C-Zl, Rome, Italy .•• 9/18/54) A. P. Wire: Startled Romans who saw a strang
        cigar-shaped object streaking over the capital flooded newspaper offices with
        caUs. Radar operators also reported picking up the missile on instruments.
        Technicians at Ciampino Airfield described the "half-cigar" as plummeting
        toward earth for more than 1000 ft., then rising at trehlendous speed before
        vanishina. (Thanks to Georae Popowitch, Barberton, Ohio. )
        An INS release by eyewitneu Michael Chinigo states: "What attracted my at·
        tention was the strange sound as it passed overhead. It was like thunder witb
        a staccato effect ••• a series of explosions that grew louder as the object got
        closer. This was followed by a dead silence as the thing stopped, or appeare
,   "   to, at a height of about 6000 ft. Suddenly it "shot upward and left an exhaust
        trail of milky white smoke. It went straight up into the sky. The Defense
I,
I
        Dept. described it as a 'clipped cone' with a smaller surface on the bottom, (
        as two s",mi-circular disks, one bigger than the other, with a bigger one on
        top. To me it seemed like an inverted sawed-oU cone. What was strange Wa!
        the objec.t's ability to 'park' in mid-air for several minutes. The radar stati,
        at Rome which picked up the 'cigar' or 'cone' said it registered for J~ minute:
        ( Thanks to Warren Morae, Palo Alto, Calif.)               "            ~

SUBJECT:                 A/NlrENTIFIED FIXING OBJECTS
                       .... .I(FIXING SAUCERS)
                                                                                                        f\~"("
                                                                                                        f\~.'("

             There appears to be no advantage to our continuing our
   yearly review of captioned matter.

             In 1958 we adopted the practice of once a year reviewing
   complaints received concerning existence of flying saucers for the
   purpose of determining whether instructions in addition to those
   already issued to the field were necessary. The last memorandum
   dated 10/2/62 pointed out that our policy concerning unidentified
   flying objects was to conduct no investigation when received in
   the field but the,iield should submit the facts immediately to
   the Air Force. oUr SAC Letters dated in 1949 and in 1952 emphasized
   the afore-mentioned policy and it does not appear that at this
   time further' instructions to the field are necessary.             ~

             During the past 12 months we have received no complaints
   regarding flying saucers and during the past 24 months only one
   complaint was received, the facts of which were immediately
   referred to the Air Force.
   ACTION:

             Since it does not appear that continuation of our yearly
   ~eview of the flying saucer situation is warranted, no further
   analysis or review of matters coming within this field will be
   conducted In:tthe future. Action with respect to any complaints
   received, of course, will follow our pOlicy.      ~ )       ~ :1 '. <# I J

                    Additionally, a news article appeared 1n           1""
          the Cincinnati Post and Times star, a daily newspaper
          of general circulation 1n the Cincinn~ti, Oh~o, area,
          under date of June 4, 1950, page 5, captioned, "Tell
          Secreta on Saucers, Expert Say~lI, which article ,is
          set forth verbatim ,as follows:
                    "Leonard Str1ngfield, Cincinnati's flying sauoer
          expert, believes the ~1ited States could take the space
          spotlight away from Russia by opening up secret tiles on
          unidentified flying objects.                                                    0


                    'It could be the start of co-operation among
          all governments of the world,1 he says. 'It all governments
          would pool their information on unidentified flying
          objects and work 1n unison on this 1t would help to ease
          world tensions ~ ,
                    1l
                    Stringfield, advertising manager for DuBois
          Chemipals, Inc., believes the unidentified flying objects
          are from outer space and that they are controlled by

                            FLYING OBJECTS
               (FLYING SAUCERS)

                  At this time there appears to be no necessity for '-'.. -
        additional instructions for the field or insertion in the FBI
                                                 ..
        Handbook or Manuals relative to flying saucers.
                                           ,     ~.   ~

                    Memorandum '10-5-59 pointed out that our policy -
        conce~ing unidentified flying objects    is to conduct DO--·.,"-,,~~--·
        investigation but bllllediately refer facts to the Air Force' --; _ ._
        (Bureau Bulletin Number 57 10-1-47): that SAC Letter NUmber
        38, 3-25-49, reiterated th 1s policy and set out data desired by -
        Air Force to be obtained when complaint received: that SAC Letter
        Number 83 8-29-52, again pointed out our policy; that Air Force
        on II-12-t7 assured tnat Bureau would be advised of any developments
        in this field of interest to us; and that there appeared to be DO
        necessity for further instructions to field or insertion in agents1
        Handbook or Manual of Instructions of our policy relating to
        flying saucers.
                  During the past twelve .onths only one complaint
        regarding flying saucers bas been referred to the Bureau. That
        immediately was turned over to Air Force, both in the field
        and at the Seat of Government.

        ACTION;
                                                                 .
                   For your information. There appears to be no necessi ty

                                                                                          "


        for additional instructions. for the field or insertion in the FBI
        Handbook or Manuals relative to flyin, saucers. This .atter .ill
